### 2026-10-07 00:25 EDT — Momentum scrolling and a bounded map backdrop

- Used the Optimize skill and researched official Lenis/GSAP documentation. Added pinned Lenis 1.3.26 using its custom-wrapper setup, wheel interpolation, touch inertia/catch, reduced-motion support, and explicit map/drawer/horizontal-guide exclusions. No bespoke scroll-physics implementation; reference: https://github.com/darkroomengineering/lenis.
- Replaced fixed background/per-scroll blur clipping with an absolute canvas and static rounded mask in content coordinates. ResizeObserver recalculates only on layout changes. The background fades over roughly 320px after the complete Guides We Love section, with an opaque tail covering filter-edge seams. Camera padding keeps camera targets centered in the established clear screen. Globe rotation pauses when that screen leaves view.
- Measured the same 45-step browser scroll before/after: mask style mutations fell from 70 to 0. Headless frame timing remained constrained (45/45 frames over 32ms in both runs), so no universal FPS or production-speed guarantee is claimed. Instrumented WebGL calls in the fresh browser counted 54 draws during a visible one-second sample and zero during an offscreen sample. One map canvas remains.
- Footer composition retains the existing PublicMapClaimDialog CTA, adds an empty 50svh section below it on home, and places smaller Privacy Policy, Terms and About controls in one bottom row. Shared Button/claim submission behavior is unchanged. Mobile keeps 44px link targets, no horizontal overflow, and the original screen footprint.
- Scoped ESLint/diff checks and 77 home/sidebar/location/performance/camera acceptance checks pass. Browser verifies momentum (351, 393, 399, 400px after a single wheel input), touch catch holds position, guide fade alignment, drawer expansion/dismissal, and footer order/space.
- Stopped the sole port-3000 listener, cleared generated .next output and restarted Node 22 in the rapid worktree. Initial cache deletion raced the exiting server; retry completed successfully once it stopped. New sole listener PID 80362, / and /map return 200 and are warmed. Source changes/auth storage and the other lane on port 3001 are preserved. Graphify refresh follows. No commit, push, deployment, or provider changes.

### 2026-10-07 — Audit public map provider cost and data transfer

- Reviewed the current Mapbox lifecycle, resource index loading/query/cache paths, organization bootstrap, detail cache and marker bitmap loading against official Mapbox pricing and Supabase pricing/storage/API documentation. No application or provider configuration changed.
- One map canvas supplies the interactive frame and blurred background; retained instances avoid initialization charges during internal interactions. Map loads, not ordinary pan/zoom or blur rendering, determine Mapbox GL JS web usage. Account usage and contracted pricing were not inspected.
- Main scaling concern: the client eagerly downloads every 200-item cursor page on mount and every five minutes while visible; eight-row UI pagination does not bound network fetching. Production endpoint headers provide five-minute shared caching with stale-while-revalidate; detail and organization caches also exist. The paged database query selects full rows and requests an exact count on every page.
- Recommended server-filtered cursor results plus viewport map data, reusable/versioned index caching, narrower database projections and cached counts, and small cached marker thumbnails instead of original image downloads. Static/lazy map initialization would be a product tradeoff; preserve the current immediate map-first experience unless requested. Documentation audit only; no tests or heavy graph regeneration needed.

### 2026-10-07 — Stabilize national nonprofit search planning

- Field publication stopped safely after search exceeded its existing two-second health budget. Saved progress: 20,045 organizations updated/read back and 401,498 categorized; exact field plan 005 remains resumable. No identity import or research restart.
- Live read-only reproduction: forcing a generic plan inside the real search RPC took 1,892.58 ms; forcing a custom plan took 16.173 ms. The equivalent generic prepared SELECT exceeded four seconds, versus 4.125 ms custom. Eight ordinary anonymous food probes currently passed at 83–480 ms; this intermittent recovery does not establish a permanent fix.
- Add function-local `plan_cache_mode=force_custom_plan` to the two directory search RPCs. No global database setting, timeout, grant, filtering, result-shape or index change. This avoids parameter-insensitive plans for text/state/cursor/category combinations. Small additional planning CPU per call is the tradeoff. See [PostgreSQL planning documentation](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/runtime-config-query.html#GUC-PLAN-CACHE-MODE).
- Disposable PostgreSQL regression passes: repeated anonymous queries under a forced-generic caller, state/EIN/text/category/cursor behavior, nested v2-to-v1 fallback, caller-setting restoration, suppression/curated exclusion and existing RLS/publication/rollback assertions. Hosted quality and required code-owner review remain release gates; migration not applied live.
- Rollout after merge: apply only `20261007190000_nonprofit_search_custom_plans.sql`, retain exact migration history, confirm both function settings and public search health, then resume permanent `filing-publication-20261007.mjs` from saved receipts. Category worker resumes after field completion. Rollback: reset only `plan_cache_mode` on both function signatures; do not reset other function configuration.
